      "description": "(user - Skill) Comprehensive AI-powered pull request review with multi-agent analysis for functional correctness, security, scalability, backward compatibility, and business logic. Use this skill when reviewing large or complex pull requests that need thorough analysis across multiple dimensions. Triggers on phrases like 'review this PR', 'check these changes', 'analyze this pull request', 'review my code', or any request involving git diffs, code review, or change validation across multiple files.",
      "template": "<skill-instruction>\nBase directory for this skill: /home/neernaredi/.agents/skills/pr-reviewer-agents/\nFile references (@path) in this skill are relative to this directory.\n\n# PR Reviewer Agents\n\nA sophisticated multi-agent system for comprehensive pull request reviews. This skill deploys specialized agents in parallel to analyze code changes across functional correctness, security, scalability, backward compatibility, and business logic dimensions.\n\n## When to Use This Skill\n\nUse this skill when you need:\n- **Thorough multi-dimensional analysis** - Not just style, but functional, security, and logic review\n- **Large PR review** - Changes across many files or complex modifications\n- **Critical code review** - Production-bound changes affecting core systems\n- **Cross-file impact assessment** - Changes that may affect other parts of the codebase\n- **Business logic validation** - Ensuring domain rules and workflows remain intact\n\n## How It Works\n\nThe skill orchestrates multiple specialized agents in parallel:\n\n```\n┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐\n│                    Review Coordinator                        │\n│                    (This instance)                           │\n└──────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┘\n                       │\n        ┌──────────────┼──────────────┬──────────────┐\n        │              │              │              │\n        ▼              ▼              ▼              ▼\n┌──────────────┐ ┌──────────┐ ┌──────────────┐ ┌──────────┐\n│   Context    │ │ Security │ │   Business   │ │  Logic   │\n│   Analyzer   │ │ Reviewer │ │    Logic     │ │Reviewer  │\n└──────────────┘ └──────────┘ └──────────────┘ └──────────┘\n        │              │              │              │\n        └──────────────┴──────────────┴──────────────┘\n                          │\n                          ▼\n              ┌───────────────────────┐\n              │    Report Synthesizer  │\n              │   (Aggregates findings)│\n              └───────────────────────┘\n```\n\nEach agent focuses on a specific dimension, allowing deep analysis without overwhelming a single context window.\n\n## Review Dimensions\n\n### 1. Context Analysis Agent\n**Purpose**: Establish full context of the change\n**Analyzes**:\n- What files changed and why\n- Entry points and affected code paths\n- Dependencies and cross-file impacts\n- Previous related changes (git history)\n- Test coverage implications\n\n### 2. Security Reviewer Agent\n**Purpose**: Identify security vulnerabilities\n**Checks**:\n- Input validation on all user inputs\n- Authentication/authorization enforcement\n- SQL injection, XSS, CSRF risks\n- Hardcoded secrets or credentials\n- Insecure dependencies\n- Data exposure in logs/errors\n\n### 3. Business Logic Reviewer Agent\n**Purpose**: Validate domain rule correctness\n**Checks**:\n- Workflow integrity and state transitions\n- Authorization logic appropriateness\n- Transaction boundaries\n- Domain invariant preservation\n- Edge case handling in business rules\n\n### 4. Logic & Correctness Reviewer Agent\n**Purpose**: Ensure code behaves as intended\n**Checks**:\n- Algorithm correctness\n- Edge case handling\n- Error handling completeness\n- Race conditions and concurrency\n- Resource management (leaks, cleanup)\n\n### 5. Scalability & Performance Reviewer Agent\n**Purpose**: Identify performance bottlenecks\n**Checks**:\n- Database query efficiency (N+1 problems)\n- Algorithmic complexity\n- Unbounded collections or recursion\n- Resource consumption patterns\n- Caching strategy appropriateness\n\n### 6. Backward Compatibility Reviewer Agent\n**Purpose**: Ensure changes don't break existing consumers\n**Checks**:\n- API contract changes (breaking vs non-breaking)\n- Database schema migrations\n- Configuration changes\n- Dependency version compatibility\n- Rollback safety\n\n### 7. Best Practices Reviewer Agent\n**Purpose**: Code quality and maintainability\n**Checks**:\n- Coding standards and conventions\n- Test coverage and quality\n- Documentation completeness\n- Code duplication\n- Naming and clarity\n\n## Usage\n\n### Basic Usage\n\n```\nReview this pull request: [PR URL or git diff]\n```\n\n### With Specific Focus\n\n```\nReview these changes with focus on security and backward compatibility: [diff]\n```\n\n### For Large PRs\n\n```\nDo a comprehensive review of this large PR across all dimensions: [diff]\n```\n\n## Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Gather Context\n\nBefore launching agents, gather:\n1. **The diff** - What changed (git diff, PR patch, or file changes)\n2. **PR description** - Intent and context from the author\n3. **Related files** - Entry points, tests, documentation\n4. **Git history** - Previous related changes\n5. **Project conventions** - Coding standards, architecture patterns\n\n### Step 2: Initialize Shared Memory\n\nCreate a shared memory file that all agents can read from and write to. This enables agents to build on each other's findings.\n\n**Create Memory File**: `/tmp/pr-review-{pr-id}-{timestamp}/shared_memory.json`\n\n```json\n{\n  \"metadata\": {\n    \"pr_id\": \"PR-123\",\n    \"review_started\": \"2026-04-08T14:30:00Z\",\n    \"files_changed\": [\"src/auth.ts\", \"src/user.ts\"],\n    \"review_status\": \"in_progress\"\n  },\n  \"context\": {\n    \"purpose\": \"Add OAuth2 authentication\",\n    \"entry_points\": [\"/api/auth/login\", \"/api/auth/callback\"],\n    \"affected_modules\": [\"authentication\", \"user_management\"],\n    \"dependencies\": [\"passport-oauth2\", \"jsonwebtoken\"]\n  },\n  \"findings\": {\n    \"security\": [],\n    \"business_logic\": [],\n    \"performance\": [],\n    \"backward_compatibility\": [],\n    \"correctness\": [],\n    \"best_practices\": []\n  },\n  \"cross_cutting_concerns\": [],\n  \"questions\": [],\n  \"progress\": {\n    \"context_analyzer\": \"pending\",\n    \"security_reviewer\": \"pending\",\n    \"business_logic_reviewer\": \"pending\",\n    \"logic_reviewer\": \"pending\",\n    \"performance_reviewer\": \"pending\",\n    \"compatibility_reviewer\": \"pending\",\n    \"best_practices_reviewer\": \"pending\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Step 3: Launch Review Agents with Memory Access\n\nDeploy specialized agents in parallel. Each agent:\n- **Reads** the shared memory before starting (to see context and previous findings)\n- **Writes** findings back to shared memory as they discover them\n- **Checks** for updates from other agents during review (for cross-cutting issues)\n\n**Memory-Aware Agent Prompt Structure**:\n```\nYou are a [Security|Business Logic|Performance|etc] Review Agent.\n\nSHARED MEMORY LOCATION: /tmp/pr-review-{pr-id}-{timestamp}/shared_memory.json\n\nINSTRUCTIONS:\n1. READ the shared memory file to understand:\n   - What has already been analyzed\n   - Findings from other agents\n   - Cross-cutting concerns to watch for\n\n2. Review the code changes for [specific dimension] issues\n\n3. WRITE your findings to shared_memory.json:\n   - Add to findings.[your_dimension]\n   - Update progress.[your_agent_name] to \"completed\"\n   - Add any cross-cutting concerns discovered\n   - Add questions that other agents might answer\n\nCHANGE CONTEXT:\n- Purpose: [from PR description]\n- Files Modified: [list]\n- Entry Points: [key functions/modules]\n\nDIFF TO REVIEW:\n```\n[patch content]\n```\n\nREVIEW CRITERIA:\n[Specific checklist for this dimension]\n\nOUTPUT FORMAT:\nReturn findings as:\n- 🔴 BLOCKER: [Critical issues that must be fixed]\n- 🟡 CONCERN: [Issues that should be addressed]\n- 🟢 SUGGESTION: [Improvements to consider]\n- ✅ PASS: [Areas that look good]\n\nFor each finding, include:\n1. File and line number\n2. Description of the issue\n3. Why it matters\n4. Suggested fix or mitigation\n5. Related findings from other agents (if applicable)\n```\n\n**Benefits of Shared Memory**:\n- **Context Accumulation**: Context analyzer's findings available to all other agents\n- **Cross-Reference**: Security findings visible to business logic reviewer\n- **Deduplication**: Agents see what others have found to avoid repetition\n- **Collaborative Discovery**: Questions from one agent can be answered by another\n- **Progress Tracking**: Coordinator can see which agents have completed\n\n**Agent Prompt Structure**:\n```\nYou are a [Security|Business Logic|Performance|etc] Review Agent.\n\nTASK: Review the following code changes for [specific dimension] issues.\n\nCHANGE CONTEXT:\n- Purpose: [from PR description]\n- Files Modified: [list]\n- Entry Points: [key functions/modules]\n\nDIFF TO REVIEW:\n```\n[patch content]\n```\n\nREVIEW CRITERIA:\n[Specific checklist for this dimension]\n\nOUTPUT FORMAT:\nReturn findings as:\n- 🔴 BLOCKER: [Critical issues that must be fixed]\n- 🟡 CONCERN: [Issues that should be addressed]\n- 🟢 SUGGESTION: [Improvements to consider]\n- ✅ PASS: [Areas that look good]\n\nFor each finding, include:\n1. File and line number\n2. Description of the issue\n3. Why it matters\n4. Suggested fix or mitigation\n```\n\n### Step 4: Synthesize Results from Shared Memory\n\nRead the shared memory file and combine findings from all agents:\n1. **Deduplicate** - Same issue found by multiple agents\n2. **Prioritize** - Blockers > Concerns > Suggestions\n3. **Cross-reference** - Security issues affecting business logic, etc.\n4. **Summarize** - Executive summary with key findings\n\n### Step 4: Generate Report\n\nProduce a structured markdown report with:\n\n```markdown\n# PR Review Report\n\n## Executive Summary\n[2-3 sentence overview: size of change, overall quality, key concerns]\n\n## Review Dimensions\n\n### 🔒 Security\n[Findings or \"No security issues identified\"]\n\n### 🏢 Business Logic\n[Findings]\n\n### ⚡ Performance & Scalability\n[Findings]\n\n### 🔄 Backward Compatibility\n[Findings]\n\n### ✅ Logic & Correctness\n[Findings]\n\n### 📋 Best Practices\n[Findings]\n\n## Action Items\n\n### Must Fix (Before Merge)\n1. [Critical issues]\n\n### Should Fix (High Priority)\n1. [Important issues]\n\n### Consider (Nice to Have)\n1. [Suggestions]\n\n## Positive Highlights\n[What was done well - good patterns, thorough tests, etc.]\n\n## Review Notes\n[Any additional context or recommendations]\n```\n\n## Handling Large PRs\n\nFor PRs exceeding context limits:\n\n1. **Chunk by File**: Group files by logical module or dependency\n2. **Prioritize Critical Paths**: Review entry points and core logic first\n3. **Incremental Review**: If multiple rounds, track what's already been reviewed\n4. **Summarize First**: Get an overview before diving into details\n\n### Token Management Strategy\n\n```\nif total_tokens < 8000:\n    Review all files in single pass\nelif total_tokens < 32000:\n    Chunk by file groups, parallel review\nelse:\n    Triage first (identify critical files)\n    Review critical files deeply\n    Review remaining files with lighter pass\n```\n\n## Review Checklists by Dimension\n\n### Security Checklist\n- [ ] All user inputs validated (server-side)\n- [ ] Authentication checks on every endpoint\n- [ ] Authorization checks for resource access\n- [ ] No hardcoded secrets, keys, or credentials\n- [ ] SQL queries use parameterized statements\n- [ ] Output is properly encoded (XSS prevention)\n- [ ] Error messages don't expose sensitive data\n- [ ] Session management is secure\n- [ ] File uploads validated (type, size, content)\n- [ ] Dependencies have no known vulnerabilities\n\n### Business Logic Checklist\n- [ ] Domain rules correctly implemented\n- [ ] State transitions are valid\n- [ ] Authorization appropriate for each operation\n- [ ] Cannot bypass workflow steps\n- [ ] Transactions properly bounded\n- [ ] Side effects are intentional and documented\n- [ ] Edge cases in business rules handled\n- [ ] Idempotency where required\n- [ ] Audit trails maintained where needed\n\n### Performance Checklist\n- [ ] No database queries in loops (N+1)\n- [ ] Proper indexes on queried columns\n- [ ] Pagination for large result sets\n- [ ] No unbounded collections or recursion\n- [ ] Caching strategy appropriate\n- [ ] No memory leaks (resources closed)\n- [ ] Async operations where beneficial\n- [ ] Algorithmic complexity appropriate\n- [ ] Rate limiting in place\n\n### Backward Compatibility Checklist\n- [ ] API changes are non-breaking (additive only)\n- [ ] Database migrations are reversible\n- [ ] New required fields have defaults\n- [ ] Deprecated features still function\n- [ ] Configuration changes handled gracefully\n- [ ] Client compatibility maintained\n- [ ] Rollback plan exists and is safe\n- [ ] Feature flags for risky changes\n\n## Tips for Effective Reviews\n\n1. **Assume Positive Intent**: Code is written by competent people solving real problems\n2. **Explain the Why**: Don't just say \"fix this\" - explain why it matters\n3. **Distinguish Blockers from Nits**: Use severity levels appropriately\n4. **Suggest, Don't Command**: Offer solutions as suggestions, not orders\n5. **Acknowledge Good Work**: Highlight what was done well\n6. **Stay Focused**: Keep feedback on the code, not the person\n7. **Be Timely**: Review promptly to avoid blocking the team\n\n## Example Agent Outputs\n\n### Security Agent Example Output\n\n```\n🔴 BLOCKER: src/auth/login.ts:45\n   Missing rate limiting on login endpoint\n   Risk: Brute force password attacks\n   Fix: Add rate limiting middleware (max 5 attempts per IP per minute)\n\n🟡 CONCERN: src/api/users.ts:78\n   User ID taken from query parameter without validation\n   Risk: Potential IDOR vulnerability\n   Fix: Validate user has permission to access requested user ID\n\n✅ PASS: src/utils/crypto.ts\n   Proper use of bcrypt for password hashing with appropriate cost factor\n```\n\n### Business Logic Agent Example Output\n\n```\n🔴 BLOCKER: src/orders/process.ts:112\n   Order can transition from CANCELLED to SHIPPED\n   Risk: Violates business rules, potential fraud\n   Fix: Add state validation preventing CANCELLED → SHIPPED transition\n\n🟡 CONCERN: src/payments/refund.ts:45\n   Refund processed without checking original payment status\n   Risk: Double refunds possible\n   Fix: Verify original payment completed and not already refunded\n\n✅ PASS: src/inventory/reserve.ts\n   Proper inventory reservation with rollback on failure\n```\n\n## Limitations\n\n1. **Context Window**: Very large PRs may need to be chunked\n2. **Dynamic Analysis**: Static review only - cannot execute code\n3. **Domain Knowledge**: May need project-specific context for full understanding\n4. **False Positives**: AI may flag patterns that are actually fine in context\n5. **Not a Replacement**: This complements human review, doesn't replace it\n\n## Integration with Git Workflows\n\nThis skill works with any git-based workflow:\n- **GitHub PRs**: Review PR diffs before merge\n- **GitLab MRs**: Analyze merge request changes\n- **Bitbucket PRs**: Review with automatic comment posting (see below)\n- **Local branches**: Review changes before pushing\n- **CI/CD**: Automated review in build pipeline\n- **Pre-commit**: Local validation before commit\n\n---\n\n## Bitbucket Integration - Automated PR Comments\n\nThe skill can automatically post review findings as comments on your Bitbucket PRs. This provides immediate, actionable feedback in the context where developers work.\n\n### Two Modes of Operation\n\n#### Mode 1: Document Mode (Default)\nGenerates a comprehensive review report as a markdown document.\n\n```\nReview this PR and generate a report: [PR details]\n```\n\n#### Mode 2: Interactive Mode (Bitbucket Comments)\nPosts findings directly as PR comments on Bitbucket.\n\n```\nReview this Bitbucket PR and post comments: \n- Workspace: myworkspace\n- Repo: myrepo\n- PR ID: 123\n- Token: [your-bitbucket-access-token]\n```\n\n### Bitbucket API Setup\n\n#### 1. Create Access Token\n\n1. Go to: `https://bitbucket.org/[your-workspace]/admin/access-tokens`\n2. Create a new access token with these permissions:\n   - `read:pullrequest:bitbucket` - Read pull requests\n   - `write:pullrequest:bitbucket` - Create/update comments\n3. Copy the token (starts with `ATBB`)\n\n#### 2. Store Your Access Token Securely\n\nYou have several options for providing the token to the skill:\n\n**Option A: Environment Variable (RECOMMENDED for CI/CD)**\n```bash\n# Add to your shell profile (~/.bashrc, ~/.zshrc, or ~/.env)\nexport BITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN=\"ATBBxxxxx...\"\n```\n\nThen reference it in the prompt:\n```\nReview this Bitbucket PR:\n- Workspace: myworkspace\n- Repo: myrepo\n- PR: 123\n- Token: ${BITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN}\n```\n\n**Option B: Direct in Prompt (Quick Testing)**\n```\nReview this Bitbucket PR:\n- Workspace: myworkspace\n- Repo: myrepo\n- PR: 123\n- Token: ATBBxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxxx\n```\n\n⚠️ **Security Note**: Only use this for testing. Never commit tokens to version control.\n\n**Option C: Config File (Local Development)**\nCreate `~/.config/pr-reviewer-agents/config.json`:\n```json\n{\n  \"bitbucket\": {\n    \"access_token\": \"ATBBxxxxx...\",\n    \"default_workspace\": \"myworkspace\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Option D: OpenCode Configuration (RECOMMENDED)**\n\nOpenCode has a built-in configuration system that supports environment variable substitution. This is the cleanest way to configure your Bitbucket credentials.\n\n**Step 1**: Create OpenCode Global Config\n\nCreate `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`:\n```json\n{\n  \"$schema\": \"https://opencode.ai/config.json\",\n  \"bitbucket\": {\n    \"access_token\": \"{env:BITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN}\",\n    \"workspace\": \"{env:BITBUCKET_WORKSPACE}\",\n    \"default_repo\": \"{env:BITBUCKET_DEFAULT_REPO}\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Step 2**: Set Environment Variables\n\nAdd to your shell profile (`~/.bashrc`, `~/.zshrc`, or `~/.env`):\n```bash\nexport BITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN=\"ATBB_your_token_here\"\nexport BITBUCKET_WORKSPACE=\"your-workspace\"\nexport BITBUCKET_DEFAULT_REPO=\"your-default-repo\"\n```\n\nThen reload:\n```bash\nsource ~/.bashrc  # or ~/.zshrc\n```\n\n**Step 3**: Use Without Token in Prompts\n\n```\nReview this Bitbucket PR:\n- Workspace: myworkspace\n- Repo: myrepo\n- PR: 123\n# Token automatically loaded from OpenCode config\n```\n\nOr even simpler:\n```\nReview Bitbucket PR 456\n# Uses all settings from OpenCode config\n```\n\n**Alternative: Project-Level OpenCode Config**\n\nCreate `opencode.json` in your project root:\n```json\n{\n  \"$schema\": \"https://opencode.ai/config.json\",\n  \"bitbucket\": {\n    \"access_token\": \"{env:BITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN}\",\n    \"workspace\": \"mycompany\",\n    \"default_repo\": \"myproject\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nThen just run:\n```\nReview Bitbucket PR 789\n```\n\n**How It Works:**\nOpenCode's `{env:VARIABLE_NAME}` syntax automatically substitutes environment variables at runtime. The skill reads from the OpenCode configuration, keeping your token secure while making it easy to use.\n\n**Option D: Project-Specific .env File**\nIn your project root, create `.env`:\n```bash\nBITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN=ATBBxxxxx...\nBITBUCKET_WORKSPACE=myworkspace\n```\n\n#### 3. Required Information\n\nTo post comments, you need:\n- **Workspace**: Your Bitbucket workspace name\n- **Repository**: The repository slug\n- **PR ID**: The pull request number\n- **Access Token**: Your Bitbucket access token (from Step 2)\n\n#### 3. Comment Types Posted\n\nThe skill will post **two types of comments**:\n\n**A. General PR Comment (Activity Tab)**\n- Overall assessment\n- Summary of findings by dimension\n- Action items prioritized\n\n**Example:**\n```\n🔴 **2 Blockers** | 🟡 **3 Concerns** | 🟢 **4 Suggestions**\n\n## Critical Issues\n1. [BLOCKER] Missing rate limiting on authentication endpoint\n2. [BLOCKER] SQL injection vulnerability in user query\n\n## Recommended Actions\n- Add rate limiting middleware (max 5 requests/minute)\n- Use parameterized queries for database operations\n\nFull report: [link to detailed review]\n```\n\n**B. Inline/File-Level Comments**\n- Specific issues on exact lines\n- Code snippets with suggestions\n- Severity indicators\n\n**Example:**\n```\n🔴 **BLOCKER**: Missing input validation\n\nFile: src/auth.ts:45\n\nCurrent code:\n  const userId = req.params.id;\n\nIssue: User input used directly in database query without validation\nRisk: SQL injection, IDOR vulnerabilities\n\nSuggested fix:\n  const userId = validateUUID(req.params.id);\n  if (!userId) {\n    return res.status(400).json({ error: \"Invalid user ID\" });\n  }\n```\n\n### Bitbucket Comment Workflow\n\n#### Step 1: Gather PR Information\n\n```bash\n# Extract from Bitbucket URL\n# https://bitbucket.org/workspace/repo/pull-requests/123\n# Workspace: workspace\n# Repo: repo\n# PR ID: 123\n```\n\n#### Step 2: Run Review with Bitbucket Integration\n\n```\nReview this Bitbucket PR and post comments:\nWorkspace: myworkspace\nRepo: myrepo  \nPR: 123\nToken: ATBBxxxxx...\n```\n\n#### Step 3: Skill Posts Comments\n\nThe skill will:\n1. Perform the full multi-agent review\n2. Generate the comprehensive report (saved locally)\n3. **Post general comment** with summary to PR activity\n4. **Post inline comments** for each specific finding:\n   - 🔴 Blockers → Top priority comments\n   - 🟡 Concerns → Secondary comments\n   - 🟢 Suggestions → Optional comments\n\n#### Step 4: Comment Organization\n\nComments are organized by:\n- **Severity**: Blockers first, then concerns, then suggestions\n- **File**: Grouped by affected file\n- **Line**: Posted on specific lines where applicable\n\n### Bitbucket API Details\n\n**Endpoint:**\n```\nPOST https://api.bitbucket.org/2.0/repositories/{workspace}/{repo}/pullrequests/{pr_id}/comments\n```\n\n**Authentication:**\n```\nAuthorization: Bearer <access_token>\nContent-Type: application/json\n```\n\n**Payload - General Comment:**\n```json\n{\n  \"content\": {\n    \"raw\": \"## PR Review Summary\\n\\n🔴 2 Blockers | 🟡 3 Concerns | 🟢 4 Suggestions\\n\\n### Critical Issues\\n1. Missing rate limiting...\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\n**Payload - Inline Comment:**\n```json\n{\n  \"content\": {\n    \"raw\": \"🔴 BLOCKER: Missing input validation\\n\\nRisk: SQL injection\\n\\nSuggested fix:\\n```\\nconst userId = validateUUID(req.params.id);\\n```\"\n  },\n  \"inline\": {\n    \"path\": \"src/auth.ts\",\n    \"from\": null,\n    \"to\": 45\n  }\n}\n```\n\n### Rate Limits and Best Practices\n\n**Bitbucket API Limits:**\n- Authenticated: 1,000 requests/hour (base)\n- Scales with seats: 1,000 + (seats-100)×10 (max 10,000)\n\n**Skill Best Practices:**\n1. **Batch blockers first**: Post critical issues before concerns\n2. **Limit inline comments**: Max 20-30 per PR to avoid noise\n3. **Consolidate where possible**: Group similar issues\n4. **Respect rate limits**: Built-in throttling (1 comment every 2 seconds)\n5. **Provide context**: Include \"why\" not just \"what\"\n\n### Using Pre-Configured Credentials (OpenCode Config)\n\nIf you've configured Bitbucket in your OpenCode config, you can use simplified prompts without specifying the token:\n\n**Prerequisites:**\n\nCreate `~/.config/opencode/opencode.json`:\n```json\n{\n  \"$schema\": \"https://opencode.ai/config.json\",\n  \"bitbucket\": {\n    \"access_token\": \"{env:BITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN}\",\n    \"workspace\": \"{env:BITBUCKET_WORKSPACE}\",\n    \"default_repo\": \"{env:BITBUCKET_DEFAULT_REPO}\"\n  }\n}\n```\n\nSet environment variables in `~/.bashrc` or `~/.zshrc`:\n```bash\nexport BITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN=\"ATBB_your_token_here\"\nexport BITBUCKET_WORKSPACE=\"your-workspace\"\nexport BITBUCKET_DEFAULT_REPO=\"your-default-repo\"\n```\n\n**Simplified Usage:**\n\n```\nReview Bitbucket PR 123 in myproject\n# Uses credentials from OpenCode config automatically\n```\n\nOr even simpler:\n```\nReview Bitbucket PR 456\n# Uses workspace and repo from OpenCode config\n```\n\n**How It Works:**\nThe skill reads the `bitbucket` configuration from OpenCode's config file. The `{env:VARIABLE_NAME}` syntax automatically substitutes your environment variables at runtime, keeping credentials secure while making them accessible to the skill.\n\n### Security Considerations\n\n**Token Security:**\n- Store token in environment variable: `BITBUCKET_ACCESS_TOKEN`\n- Never commit tokens to code\n- Use repository-specific tokens (not personal)\n- Rotate tokens regularly\n- For OpenCode: Add to `~/.bashrc` or `~/.zshrc`, not to project files\n\n**Comment Content:**\n- No sensitive data in comments (passwords, keys)\n- No internal URLs or infrastructure details\n- Focus on code issues, not personal criticism\n\n### Configuration Options\n\n**Control Which Comments Get Posted:**\n\n```\nReview Bitbucket PR and post:\n- Workspace: myworkspace\n- Repo: myrepo\n- PR: 123\n- Token: ATBBxxxxx\n- Post blockers: yes\n- Post concerns: yes\n- Post suggestions: no\n- Max inline comments: 20\n```\n\n**Skip Inline Comments (Summary Only):**\n\n```\nReview Bitbucket PR and post summary comment only:\n- Workspace: myworkspace\n- Repo: myrepo\n- PR: 123\n- Token: ATBBxxxxx\n```\n\n### Example Complete Workflow\n\n**User Request:**\n```\nReview this Bitbucket PR:\n- Workspace: acme-corp\n- Repo: payment-service\n- PR: 456\n- Token: ATBBxxxxx\n- Post all findings as comments\n```\n\n**Skill Execution:**\n1.       "description": "(user - Skill) Generate Excalidraw diagrams from natural language descriptions. Use when asked to \"create a diagram\", \"make a flowchart\", \"visualize a process\", \"draw a system architecture\", \"create a mind map\", or \"generate an Excalidraw file\". Supports flowcharts, relationship diagrams, mind maps, and system architecture diagrams. Outputs .excalidraw JSON files that can be opened directly in Excalidraw.",
      "template": "<skill-instruction>\nBase directory for this skill: /home/neernaredi/.agents/skills/excalidraw-diagram-generator/\nFile references (@path) in this skill are relative to this directory.\n\n# Excalidraw Diagram Generator\n\nA skill for generating Excalidraw-format diagrams from natural language descriptions. This skill helps create visual representations of processes, systems, relationships, and ideas without manual drawing.\n\n## When to Use This Skill\n\nUse this skill when users request:\n\n- \"Create a diagram showing...\"\n- \"Make a flowchart for...\"\n- \"Visualize the process of...\"\n- \"Draw the system architecture of...\"\n- \"Generate a mind map about...\"\n- \"Create an Excalidraw file for...\"\n- \"Show the relationship between...\"\n- \"Diagram the workflow of...\"\n\n**Supported diagram types:**\n- 📊 **Flowcharts**: Sequential processes, workflows, decision trees\n- 🔗 **Relationship Diagrams**: Entity relationships, system components, dependencies\n- 🧠 **Mind Maps**: Concept hierarchies, brainstorming results, topic organization\n- 🏗️ **Architecture Diagrams**: System design, module interactions, data flow\n- 📈 **Data Flow Diagrams (DFD)**: Data flow visualization, data transformation processes\n- 🏊 **Business Flow (Swimlane)**: Cross-functional workflows, actor-based process flows\n- 📦 **Class Diagrams**: Object-oriented design, class structures and relationships\n- 🔄 **Sequence Diagrams**: Object interactions over time, message flows\n- 🗃️ **ER Diagrams**: Database entity relationships, data models\n\n## Prerequisites\n\n- Clear description of what should be visualized\n- Identification of key entities, steps, or concepts\n- Understanding of relationships or flow between elements\n\n## Step-by-Step Workflow\n\n### Step 1: Understand the Request\n\nAnalyze the user's description to determine:\n1. **Diagram type** (flowchart, relationship, mind map, architecture)\n2. **Key elements** (entities, steps, concepts)\n3. **Relationships** (flow, connections, hierarchy)\n4. **Complexity** (number of elements)\n\n### Step 2: Choose the Appropriate Diagram Type\n\n| User Intent | Diagram Type | Example Keywords |\n|-------------|--------------|------------------|\n| Process flow, steps, procedures | **Flowchart** | \"workflow\", \"process\", \"steps\", \"procedure\" |\n| Connections, dependencies, associations | **Relationship Diagram** | \"relationship\", \"connections\", \"dependencies\", \"structure\" |\n| Concept hierarchy, brainstorming | **Mind Map** | \"mind map\", \"concepts\", \"ideas\", \"breakdown\" |\n| System design, components | **Architecture Diagram** | \"architecture\", \"system\", \"components\", \"modules\" |\n| Data flow, transformation processes | **Data Flow Diagram (DFD)** | \"data flow\", \"data processing\", \"data transformation\" |\n| Cross-functional processes, actor responsibilities | **Business Flow (Swimlane)** | \"business process\", \"swimlane\", \"actors\", \"responsibilities\" |\n| Object-oriented design, class structures | **Class Diagram** | \"class\", \"inheritance\", \"OOP\", \"object model\" |\n| Interaction sequences, message flows | **Sequence Diagram** | \"sequence\", \"interaction\", \"messages\", \"timeline\" |\n| Database design, entity relationships | **ER Diagram** | \"database\", \"entity\", \"relationship\", \"data model\" |\n\n### Step 3: Extract Structured Information\n\n**For Flowcharts:**\n- List of sequential steps\n- Decision points (if any)\n- Start and end points\n\n**For Relationship Diagrams:**\n- Entities/nodes (name + optional description)\n- Relationships between entities (from → to, with label)\n\n**For Mind Maps:**\n- Central topic\n- Main branches (3-6 recommended)\n- Sub-topics for each branch (optional)\n\n**For Data Flow Diagrams (DFD):**\n- Data sources and destinations (external entities)\n- Processes (data transformations)\n- Data stores (databases, files)\n- Data flows (arrows showing data movement from left-to-right or from top-left to bottom-right)\n- **Important**: Do not represent process order, only data flow\n\n**For Business Flow (Swimlane):**\n- Actors/roles (departments, systems, people) - displayed as header columns\n- Process lanes (vertical lanes under each actor)\n- Process boxes (activities within each lane)\n- Flow arrows (connecting process boxes, including cross-lane handoffs)\n\n**For Class Diagrams:**\n- Classes with names\n- Attributes with visibility (+, -, #)\n- Methods with visibility and parameters\n- Relationships: inheritance (solid line + white triangle), implementation (dashed line + white triangle), association (solid line), dependency (dashed line), aggregation (solid line + white diamond), composition (solid line + filled diamond)\n- Multiplicity notations (1, 0..1, 1..*, *)\n\n**For Sequence Diagrams:**\n- Objects/actors (arranged horizontally at top)\n- Lifelines (vertical lines from each object)\n- Messages (horizontal arrows between lifelines)\n- Synchronous messages (solid arrow), asynchronous messages (dashed arrow)\n- Return values (dashed arrows)\n- Activation boxes (rectangles on lifelines during execution)\n- Time flows from top to bottom\n\n**For ER Diagrams:**\n- Entities (rectangles with entity names)\n- Attributes (listed inside entities)\n- Primary keys (underlined or marked with PK)\n- Foreign keys (marked with FK)\n- Relationships (lines connecting entities)\n- Cardinality: 1:1 (one-to-one), 1:N (one-to-many), N:M (many-to-many)\n- Junction/associative entities for many-to-many relationships (dashed rectangles)\n\n### Step 4: Generate the Excalidraw JSON\n\nCreate the `.excalidraw` file with appropriate elements:\n\n**Available element types:**\n- `rectangle`: Boxes for entities, steps, concepts\n- `ellipse`: Alternative shapes for emphasis\n- `diamond`: Decision points\n- `arrow`: Directional connections\n- `text`: Labels and annotations\n\n**Key properties to set:**\n- **Position**: `x`, `y` coordinates\n- **Size**: `width`, `height`\n- **Style**: `strokeColor`, `backgroundColor`, `fillStyle`\n- **Font**: `fontFamily: 5` (Excalifont - **required for all text elements**)\n- **Text**: Embedded text for labels\n- **Connections**: `points` array for arrows\n\n**Important**: All text elements must use `fontFamily: 5` (Excalifont) for consistent visual appearance.\n\n### Step 5: Format the Output\n\nStructure the complete Excalidraw file:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"type\": \"excalidraw\",\n  \"version\": 2,\n  \"source\": \"https://excalidraw.com\",\n  \"elements\": [\n    // Array of diagram elements\n  ],\n  \"appState\": {\n    \"viewBackgroundColor\": \"#ffffff\",\n    \"gridSize\": 20\n  },\n  \"files\": {}\n}\n```\n\n### Step 6: Save and Provide Instructions\n\n1. Save as `<descriptive-name>.excalidraw`\n2. Inform user how to open:\n   - Visit https://excalidraw.com\n   - Click \"Open\" or drag-and-drop the file\n   - Or use Excalidraw VS Code extension\n\n## Best Practices\n\n### Element Count Guidelines\n\n| Diagram Type | Recommended Count | Maximum |\n|--------------|-------------------|---------|\n| Flowchart steps | 3-10 | 15 |\n| Relationship entities | 3-8 | 12 |\n| Mind map branches | 4-6 | 8 |\n| Mind map sub-topics per branch | 2-4 | 6 |\n\n### Layout Tips\n\n1. **Start positions**: Center important elements, use consistent spacing\n2. **Spacing**: \n   - Horizontal gap: 200-300px between elements\n   - Vertical gap: 100-150px between rows\n3. **Colors**: Use consistent color scheme\n   - Primary elements: Light blue (`#a5d8ff`)\n   - Secondary elements: Light green (`#b2f2bb`)\n   - Important/Central: Yellow (`#ffd43b`)\n   - Alerts/Warnings: Light red (`#ffc9c9`)\n4. **Text sizing**: 16-24px for readability\n5. **Font**: Always use `fontFamily: 5` (Excalifont) for all text elements\n6. **Arrow style**: Use straight arrows for simple flows, curved for complex relationships\n\n### Complexity Management\n\n**If user request has too many elements:**\n- Suggest breaking into multiple diagrams\n- Focus on main elements first\n- Offer to create detailed sub-diagrams\n\n**Example response:**\n```\n\"Your request includes 15 components. For clarity, I recommend:\n1. High-level architecture diagram (6 main components)\n2. Detailed diagram for each subsystem\n\nWould you like me to start with the high-level view?\"\n```\n\n## Example Prompts and Responses\n\n### Example 1: Simple Flowchart\n\n**User:** \"Create a flowchart for user registration\"\n\n**Agent generates:**\n1. Extract steps: \"Enter email\" → \"Verify email\" → \"Set password\" → \"Complete\"\n2. Create flowchart with 4 rectangles + 3 arrows\n3. Save as `user-registration-flow.excalidraw`\n\n### Example 2: Relationship Diagram\n\n**User:** \"Diagram the relationship between User, Post, and Comment entities\"\n\n**Agent generates:**\n1. Entities: User, Post, Comment\n2. Relationships: User → Post (\"creates\"), User → Comment (\"writes\"), Post → Comment (\"contains\")\n3. Save as `user-content-relationships.excalidraw`\n\n### Example 3: Mind Map\n\n**User:** \"Mind map about machine learning concepts\"\n\n**Agent generates:**\n1. Center: \"Machine Learning\"\n2. Branches: Supervised Learning, Unsupervised Learning, Reinforcement Learning, Deep Learning\n3. Sub-topics under each branch\n4. Save as `machine-learning-mindmap.excalidraw`\n\n## Troubleshooting\n\n| Issue | Solution |\n|-------|----------|\n| Elements overlap | Increase spacing between coordinates |\n| Text doesn't fit in boxes | Increase box width or reduce font size |\n| Too many elements | Break into multiple diagrams |\n| Unclear layout | Use grid layout (rows/columns) or radial layout (mind maps) |\n| Colors inconsistent | Define color palette upfront based on element types |\n\n## Advanced Techniques\n\n### Grid Layout (for Relationship Diagrams)\n```javascript\nconst columns = Math.ceil(Math.sqrt(entityCount));\nconst x = startX + (index % columns) * horizontalGap;\nconst y = startY + Math.floor(index / columns) * verticalGap;\n```\n\n### Radial Layout (for Mind Maps)\n```javascript\nconst angle = (2 * Math.PI * index) / branchCount;\nconst x = centerX + radius * Math.cos(angle);\nconst y = centerY + radius * Math.sin(angle);\n```\n\n### Auto-generated IDs\nUse timestamp + random string for unique IDs:\n```javascript\nconst id = Date.now().toString(36) + Math.random().toString(36).substr(2);\n```\n\n## Output Format\n\nAlways provide:\n1. ✅ Complete `.excalidraw` JSON file\n2. 📊 Summary of what was created\n3. 📝 Element count\n4. 💡 Instructions for opening/editing\n\n**Example summary:**\n```\nCreated: user-workflow.excalidraw\nType: Flowchart\nElements: 7 rectangles, 6 arrows, 1 title text\nTotal: 14 elements\n\nTo view:\n1. Visit https://excalidraw.com\n2. Drag and drop user-workflow.excalidraw\n3. Or use File → Open in Excalidraw VS Code extension\n```\n\n## Validation Checklist\n\nBefore delivering the diagram:\n- [ ] All elements have unique IDs\n- [ ] Coordinates prevent overlapping\n- [ ] Text is readable (font size 16+)\n- [ ] **All text elements use `fontFamily: 5` (Excalifont)**\n- [ ] Arrows connect logically\n- [ ] Colors follow consistent scheme\n- [ ] File is valid JSON\n- [ ] Element count is reasonable (<20 for clarity)\n\n## Icon Libraries (Optional Enhancement)\n\nFor specialized diagrams (e.g., AWS/GCP/Azure architecture diagrams), you can use pre-made icon libraries from Excalidraw. ...\n```\n\n### AI Assistant Workflow\n\n**When icon libraries are available in `libraries/`:**\n\n**RECOMMENDED APPROACH: Use Python Scripts (Efficient & Reliable)**\n\nThe repository includes Python # Arrow with label\n   python scripts/add-arrow.py diagram.excalidraw 300 250 500 300 --label \"HTTPS\"\n   \n   # Dashed arrow with custom color\n   python scripts/add-arrow.py diagram.excalidraw 400 ELB\n   python scripts/add-arrow.py my-diagram.excalidraw 400 330 500 350  # ELB → EC2\n   python scripts/add-arrow.py my-diagram.excalidraw 500 380 600 400  # EC2 → RDS\n   ```\n\n**Benefits of Python Script Approach**:\n- ✅ **No token consumption**: Icon JSON data (200-1000 lines each) never enters AI context\n- ✅ **Accurate transformations**: Coordinate calculations handled deterministically\n- ✅ **ID management**: Automatic UUID generation prevents conflicts\n- ✅ **Reliable**: No risk of coordinate miscalculation or ID collision\n- ✅ **Fast**: Direct file manipulation, no parsing overhead\n- ✅ **Reusable**: Works with any Excalidraw library you provide\n\n**ALTERNATIVE: Manual Icon Integration (Not Recommended)**\n\nOnly use this if Python scripts are unavailable:\n\n1. **Check for libraries**: \n   ```\n   List directory: skills/excalidraw-diagram-generator/libraries/\n   Look for subdirectories containing reference.md files\n   ```\n\n2. **Read reference.md**:\n   ```\n   Open: libraries/<library-name>/reference.md\n   This is lightweight (typically <300 lines) and lists all available icons\n   ```\n\n3. **Find relevant icons**:\n   ```\n   Search the reference.md table for icon names matching diagram needs\n   Example: For AWS diagram with EC2, S3, Lambda → Find \"EC2\", \"S3\", \"Lambda\" in table\n   ```\n\n4. **Load specific icon data** (WARNING: Large files):\n   ```\n   Read ONLY the needed icon files:\n   - libraries/aws-architecture-icons/icons/EC2.json (200-300 lines)\n   - libraries/aws-architecture-icons/icons/S3.json (200-300 lines)\n   - libraries/aws-architecture-icons/icons/Lambda.json (200-300 lines)\n   Note: Each icon file is 200-1000 lines - this consumes significant tokens\n   ```\n\n5. **Extract and transform elements**:\n   ```\n   Each icon JSON contains an \"elements\" array\n   Calculate bounding box (min_x, min_y, max_x, max_y)\n   Apply offset to all x/y coordinates\n   Generate new unique IDs for all elements\n   Update groupIds references\n   Copy transformed elements into your diagram\n   ```\n\n6. **Position icons and add connections**:\n   ```\n   Adjust x/y coordinates to position icons correctly in the diagram\n   Update IDs to ensure uniqueness across diagram\n   Add connecting arrows and labels as needed\n   ```\n\n**Manual Integration Challenges**:\n- ⚠️ High token consumption (200-1000 lines per icon × number of icons)\n- ⚠️ Complex coordinate transformation calculations\n- ⚠️ Risk of ID collision if not handled carefully\n- ⚠️ Time-consuming for diagrams with many icons\n\n### Example: Creating AWS Diagram with Icons\n\n**Request**: \"Create an AWS architecture diagram with Internet Gateway, VPC, ELB, EC2, and RDS\"\n\n**Recommended Workflow (using Python scripts)**:\n**Request**: \"Create an AWS architecture diagram with Internet Gateway, VPC, ELB, EC2, and RDS\"\n\n**Recommended Workflow (using Python scripts)**:\n\n```bash\n# Step 1: Create base diagram file with title\n# Create my-aws-diagram.excalidraw with basic structure (title, etc.)\n\n# Step 2: Check icon availability\n# Read: libraries/aws-architecture-icons/reference.md\n# Confirm icons exist: Internet-gateway, VPC, ELB, EC2, RDS\n\n# Step 3: Add icons with Python script\npython scripts/add-icon-to-diagram.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw \"Internet-gateway\" 150 100 --label \"Internet Gateway\"\npython scripts/add-icon-to-diagram.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw VPC 200 200\npython scripts/add-icon-to-diagram.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw ELB 350 250 --label \"Load Balancer\"\npython scripts/add-icon-to-diagram.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw EC2 500 300 --label \"Web Server\"\npython scripts/add-icon-to-diagram.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw RDS 650 350 --label \"Database\"\n\n# Step 4: Add connecting arrows\npython scripts/add-arrow.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw 200 150 250 200  # Internet → VPC\npython scripts/add-arrow.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw 265 230 350 250  # VPC → ELB\npython scripts/add-arrow.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw 415 280 500 300  # ELB → EC2\npython scripts/add-arrow.py my-aws-diagram.excalidraw 565 330 650 350 --label \"SQL\" --style dashed\n\n# Result: Complete diagram with professional AWS icons, labels, and connections\n```\n\n**Benefits**:\n- No manual coordinate calculation\n- No token consumption for icon data\n- Deterministic, reliable results\n- Easy to iterate and adjust positions\n\n**Alternative Workflow (manual, if scripts unavailable)**:\n1. Check: `libraries/aws-architecture-icons/reference.md` exists → Yes\n2. Read reference.md → Find entries for Internet-gateway, VPC, ELB, EC2, RDS\n3. Load:\n   - `icons/Internet-gateway.json` (298 lines)\n   - `icons/VPC.json` (550 lines)\n   - `icons/ELB.json` (363 lines)\n   - `icons/EC2.json` (231 lines) \n   - `icons/RDS.json` (similar size)\n   **Total: ~2000+ lines of JSON to process**\n4. Extract elements from each JSON\n5. Calculate bounding boxes and offsets for each icon\n6. Transform all coordinates (x, y) for positioning\n7. Generate unique IDs for all elements\n8. Add arrows showing data flow\n9. Add text labels\n10. Generate final `.excalidraw` file\n\n**Challenges with manual approach**:\n- High token consumption (~2000-5000 lines)\n- Complex coordinate math\n- Risk of ID conflicts\n\n### Supported Icon Libraries (Examples — verify availability)\n\n- This workflow works with any valid `.excalidrawlib` file you provide.\n- Examples of library categories you may find on https://libraries.excalidraw.com/:\n   - Cloud service icons\n   - Kubernetes / infrastructure icons\n   - UI / Material icons\n   - Flowchart / diagram symbols\n   - Network diagram icons\n- Availability and naming can change; verify exact library names on the site before use.\n\n### Fallback: No Icons Available\n\n**If no icon libraries are set up:**\n- Create diagrams using basic shapes (rectangles, ellipses, arrows)\n- Use color coding and text labels to distinguish components\n- Inform user they can add icons later or set up libraries for future diagrams\n- The diagram will still be functional and clear, just less visually polished\n\n## References\n\nSee bundled references for:\n- `references/excalidraw-schema.md` - Complete Excalidraw JSON schema\n- `references/element-types.md` - Detailed element type specifications\n- `templates/flowchart-template.json` - Basic flowchart starter\n- `templates/relationship-template.json` - Relationship diagram starter\n- `templates/mindmap-template.json` - Mind map starter\n- `scripts/split-excalidraw-library.py` - Tool to split `.excalidrawlib` files\n- `scripts/README.md` - Documentation for library tools\n- `scripts/.gitignore` - Prevents local Python artifacts from being committed\n\n## Limitations\n\n- Complex curves are simplified to straight/basic curved lines\n- Hand-drawn roughness is set to default (1)\n- No embedded images support in auto-generation\n- Maximum recommended elements: 20 per diagram\n- No automatic collision detection (use spacing guidelines)\n\n## Future Enhancements\n\nPotential improvements:\n- Auto-layout optimization algorithms\n- Import from Mermaid/PlantUML syntax\n- Template library expansion\n- Interactive editing after generation\n</skill-instruction>\n\n<user-request>\n$ARGUMENTS\n</user-request>"

Skill\n\nA comprehensive, multi-agent pull request review system that analyzes code changes across multiple dimensions: security, performance, architecture, business logic, and backward compatibility.\n\n## When to Use This Skill\n\nUse this skill when:\n- Reviewing pull requests or merge requests of any size\n- Changes involve security-sensitive code (authentication, authorization, data handling)\n- Modifications to public APIs, interfaces, or contracts\n- Database schema or migration changes\n- Performance-critical code paths\n- Business logic changes (workflows, state machines, calculations)\n- You need thorough analysis beyond automated linting\n\n## What This Skill Provides\n\nUnlike basic code review tools that focus on style and syntax, this skill provides:\n\n1. **Multi-dimensional Analysis**: Parallel review by specialized agents for security, performance, architecture, business logic, and backward compatibility\n2. **Context-Aware Review**: Analyzes git history, related files, and codebase patterns to understand the full impact of changes\n3. **Business Logic Validation**: Identifies potential issues in domain logic, invariants, and workflow integrity\n4. **Cross-Reference Synthesis**: Detects conflicts between recommendations (e.g., security fix that hurts performance)\n5. **Structured Output**: Categorized findings with severity levels, evidence, and actionable recommendations\n\n## Workflow Overview\n\n```\nPhase 1: Context Gathering (Parallel)\n  → Codebase Context Agent: Maps project structure, identifies patterns\n  → History Agent: Analyzes git history, related commits, previous changes\n  → Diff Analyzer: Parses changes, identifies affected components\n\nPhase 2: Multi-Dimensional Review (Parallel)\n  → Security Agent: Vulnerability scan, OWASP patterns, data flow analysis\n  → Performance Agent: Algorithmic complexity, resource management, N+1 queries\n  → Architecture Agent: Pattern compliance, layer violations, abstraction quality\n  → Business Logic Agent: Domain rule validation, invariant checking, workflow integrity\n  → Backward Compatibility Agent: Breaking changes, API contract violations\n\nPhase 3: Synthesis\n  → Cross-reference findings across dimensions\n  → Prioritize by severity and business impact\n  → Resolve conflicting recommendations\n\nPhase 4: Report Generation\n  → Structured markdown report with categorized findings\n```\n\n## Usage\n\n### Basic Usage\n\n```\n/review-work\nReview this PR: https://github.com/org/repo/pull/123\n```\n\n### With Context\n\n```\n/review-work\nReview the changes in branch feature/payment-gateway against main\nThis PR adds a new payment processing workflow with Stripe integration\nFocus areas: security of payment data, backward compatibility with existing orders\n```\n\n### Local Git Workflow\n\n```\n/review-work\nReview the current branch changes against develop\nContext: This modifies the user authentication flow\n```\n\n## Output Format\n\nThe skill produces a structured markdown report:\n\n```markdown\n# PR Review Report\n\n## Summary\n- **Files Changed**: 12\n- **Lines Changed**: +450, `src/payments/query.js:45`\n  - **Evidence**: User input directly interpolated into SQL string\n  - **Recommendation**: Use parameterized queries\n\n### 🟡 Medium\n- **Issue**: Missing rate limiting on new endpoint\n  - **Location**: `src/api/routes/payment.js:23`\n  - **Evidence**: No rate limiting middleware applied\n  - **Recommendation**: Add express-rate-limit with appropriate thresholds\n\n## Performance Review\n\n### 🟡 Medium\n- **Issue**: Potential N+1 query in order fetching\n  - **Location**: `src/orders/service.js:78`\n  - **Evidence**: Loop iterates over customers, queries orders for each\n  - **Recommendation**: Use JOIN or IN clause with batched query\n\n## Business Logic Review\n\n### 🟡 Medium\n- **Issue**: Order status transition validation missing\n  - **Location**: `src/orders/state-machine.js:34`\n  - **Evidence**: Direct status assignment without transition validation\n  - **Recommendation**: Implement state machine with valid transition rules\n  - **Context**: Previous incidents (PR #89, INC-2024-03) caused by invalid transitions\n\n## Backward Compatibility\n\n### 🟢 Low\n- API endpoint changes are backward compatible\n- Database migration is safe (adds nullable column)\n\n## Action Items\n1. [CRITICAL] Fix SQL injection before merging\n2. [MEDIUM] Add rate limiting to payment endpoint\n3. [MEDIUM] Optimize order fetching query\n4. [LOW] Consider adding transition validation\n```\n\n## Review Dimensions\n\n### 1. Security Review\n\nChecks for:\n- Injection vulnerabilities (SQL, NoSQL, Command, LDAP)\n- Authentication/authorization flaws\n- Sensitive data exposure (secrets, PII)\n- Cross-site scripting (XSS)\n- Insecure deserialization\n- Security misconfigurations\n- Cross-file data flow analysis\n\n**Evidence Format**: Specific code snippets showing the vulnerability with CWE references.\n\n### 2. Performance Review\n\nChecks for:\n- Database query patterns (N+1, missing indexes, unbounded results)\n- Algorithmic complexity (O(n²) or worse on large datasets)\n- Resource leaks (connections, file handles, memory)\n- Synchronous blocking in async contexts\n- Missing pagination on list endpoints\n- Caching strategy appropriateness\n\n**Evidence Format**: Time/space complexity analysis, query execution patterns.\n\n### 3. Architecture Review\n\nChecks for:\n- Layer violations (business logic in controllers)\n- Abstraction quality (appropriate level of abstraction)\n- Dependency injection usage\n- Repository pattern compliance\n- Service boundary integrity\n- State management patterns\n\n**Evidence Format**: References to architectural patterns and where violations occur.\n\n### 4. Business Logic Review\n\nChecks for:\n- Domain rule violations\n- State machine integrity\n- Workflow completeness\n- Authorization logic appropriateness\n- Transaction boundaries\n- Race conditions in concurrent operations\n- Edge case handling\n\n**Evidence Format**: Description of expected vs actual behavior, business rule references.\n\n### 5. Backward Compatibility Review\n\nChecks for:\n- API breaking changes (removed fields, added required params, type changes)\n- Database schema compatibility\n- Configuration changes\n- Behavioral changes in existing features\n- Migration path for breaking changes\n\n**Evidence Format**: Diff of contracts/schemas with compatibility analysis.\n\n## Large PR Handling\n\nFor PRs with 500+ lines of changes:\n\n1. **Intelligent Chunking**: Changes are grouped by logical components (not just files)\n2. **Parallel Processing**: Multiple agents review chunks simultaneously\n3. **Cross-Chunk Validation**: After parallel review, checks for issues spanning multiple chunks\n4. **Incremental Reporting**: Findings reported as they're discovered\n\n## Context Gathering\n\nThe skill automatically gathers:\n\n1. **Codebase Context**:\n   - Project structure and conventions\n   - Similar implementations (pattern matching)\n   - Test coverage of affected areas\n\n2. **Git History**:\n   - Related previous changes\n   - Authors of original code (for context)\n   - Previous incidents or bug fixes in the area\n   - Commit message patterns\n\n3. **Change Context**:\n   - Type of changes (feature, bugfix, refactor)\n   - Files modified and their relationships\n   - Cross-file dependencies\n\n## Severity Levels\n\n- **🔴 Critical**: Must fix before merging. Security vulnerabilities, data loss risks, critical functionality breaks.\n- **🟠 High**: Should fix before merging. Significant performance issues, architectural violations, potential bugs.\n- **🟡 Medium**: Fix when convenient. Minor issues, code quality improvements, suggestions.\n- **🟢 Low**: Informational. Style preferences, minor optimizations, notes.\n\n## Best Practices\n\n### For Reviewers\n\n1. **Start with Summary**: Read the overall assessment first\n2. **Prioritize by Severity**: Address critical and high issues first\n3. **Check Business Logic**: Pay special attention to domain logic findings\n4. **Consider Context**: Review agents' evidence and recommendations together\n\n### For PR Authors\n\n1. **Provide Context**: Include description of what and why in PR description\n2. **Size Appropriately**: Keep PRs under 400 lines when possible\n3. **Self-Review First**: Run the skill yourself before requesting review\n4. **Address Blockers**: Fix critical/high severity issues promptly\n\n## Integration\n\nThis skill works with:\n- **GitHub**: PR URLs, local clones with GitHub remotes\n- **GitLab**: MR URLs, local clones with GitLab remotes\n- **Bitbucket**: PR URLs, local clones\n- **Local Git**: Any git repository with branches\n\n## Limitations\n\n1. **Business Logic**: Cannot fully validate domain correctness without domain-specific rules. Provides pattern-based analysis only.\n2. **Large Refactors**: May have reduced accuracy on PRs changing 1000+ lines\n3. **Context Window**: Very large files may be summarized rather than fully analyzed\n4. **No Runtime Analysis**: Static analysis only; cannot detect runtime-only issues\n\n## Feedback Loop\n\nAfter using this skill:\n1. Saves to UBIQUITOUS_LANGUAGE.md. Use when user wants to define domain terms, build a glossary, harden terminology, create a ubiquitous language, or mentions \"domain model\" or \"DDD\".",
      "template": "<skill-instruction>\nBase directory for this skill: /home/neernaredi/.agents/skills/ubiquitous-language/\nFile references (@path) in this skill are relative to this directory.\n\n# Ubiquitous Language\n\nExtract and formalize domain terminology from the current conversation into a consistent glossary, saved to a local file.\n\n## Process\n\n1. **Scan the conversation** for domain-relevant nouns, verbs, and concepts\n2. **Identify problems**:\n   - Same word used for different concepts (ambiguity)\n   - Different words used for the same concept (synonyms)\n   - Vague or overloaded terms\n3. **Propose a canonical glossary** with opinionated term choices\n4. **Write to `UBIQUITOUS_LANGUAGE.md`** in the working directory using the format below\n5. **Output a summary** inline in the conversation\n\n## Output Format\n\nWrite a `UBIQUITOUS_LANGUAGE.md` file with this structure:\n\n```md\n# Ubiquitous Language\n\n## Order lifecycle\n\n| Term        | Definition                                              | Aliases to avoid      |\n| ----------- | ------------------------------------------------------- | --------------------- |\n| **Order**   | A customer's request to purchase one or more items      | Purchase, transaction |\n| **Invoice** | A request for payment sent to a customer after delivery | Bill, payment request |\n\n## People\n\n| Term         | Definition                                  | Aliases to avoid       |\n| ------------ | ------------------------------------------- | ---------------------- |\n| **Customer** | A person or organization that places orders | Client, buyer, account |\n| **User**     | An authentication identity in the system    | Login, account         |\n\n## Relationships\n\n- An **Invoice** belongs to exactly one **Customer**\n- An **Order** produces one or more **Invoices**\n\n## Example dialogue\n\n> **Dev:** \"When a **Customer** places an **Order**, do we create the **Invoice** immediately?\"\n> **Domain expert:** \"No — an **Invoice** is only generated once a **Fulfillment** is confirmed. A single **Order** can produce multiple **Invoices** if items ship in separate **Shipments**.\"\n> **Dev:** \"So if a **Shipment** is cancelled before dispatch, no **Invoice** exists for it?\"\n> **Domain expert:** \"Exactly. The **Invoice** lifecycle is tied to the **Fulfillment**, not the **Order**.\"\n\n## Flagged ambiguities\n\n- \"account\" was used to mean both **Customer** and **User** — these are distinct concepts: a **Customer** places orders, while a **User** is an authentication identity that may or may not represent a **Customer**.\n```\n\n## Rules\n\n- **Be opinionated.** When multiple words exist for the same concept, pick the best one and list the others as aliases to avoid.\n- **Flag conflicts explicitly.** If a term is used ambiguously in the conversation, call it out in the \"Flagged ambiguities\" section with a clear recommendation.\n- **Only include terms relevant for domain experts.** Skip the names of modules or classes unless they have meaning in the domain language.\n- **Keep definitions tight.** One sentence max. Define what it IS, not what it does.\n- **Show relationships.** Use bold term names and express cardinality where obvious.\n- **Only include domain terms.** Skip generic programming concepts (array, function, endpoint) unless they have domain-specific meaning.\n- **Group terms into multiple tables** when natural clusters emerge (e.g. by subdomain, lifecycle, or actor). Each group gets its own heading and table. If all terms belong to a single cohesive domain, one table is fine — don't force groupings.\n- **Write an example dialogue.** A short conversation (3-5 exchanges) between a dev and a domain expert that demonstrates how the terms interact naturally. The dialogue should clarify boundaries between related concepts and show terms being used precisely.\n\n<example>\n\n## Example dialogue\n\n> **Dev:** \"How do I test the **sync service** without Docker?\"\n\n> **Domain expert:** \"Provide the **filesystem layer** instead of the **Docker layer**. It implements the same **Sandbox service** interface but uses a local directory as the **sandbox**.\"\n\n> **Dev:** \"So **sync-in** still creates a **bundle** and unpacks it?\"\n\n> **Domain expert:** \"Exactly. The **sync service** doesn't know which layer it's talking to. It calls `exec` and `copyIn` — the **filesystem layer** just runs those as local shell commands.\"\n\n</example>\n\n## Re-running\n\nWhen invoked again in the same conversation:\n\n1. Read the existing `UBIQUITOUS_LANGUAGE.md`\n2. Incorporate any new terms from subsequent discussion\n3. Update definitions if understanding has evolved\n4. Re-flag any new ambiguities\n5. Rewrite the example dialogue to incorporate new terms\n</skill-instruction>\n\n<user-request>\n$ARGUMENTS\n</user-request>"

Skills are modular packages that extend agent capabilities with specialized knowledge, workflows, and tools.\n\n**Key commands:**\n\n- `npx skills find [query]` - Search for skills interactively or by keyword\n- `npx skills add <package>` - Install a skill from GitHub or other sources\n- `npx skills check` - Check for skill updates\n- `npx skills update` - Update all installed skills\n\n**Browse skills at:** https://skills.sh/\n\n## How to Help Users Find Skills\n\n### Step 1: Understand What They Need\n\nWhen a user asks for help with something, identify:\n\n1. The leaderboard ranks skills by total installs, surfacing the most popular and battle-tested options.\n\nFor example, top skills for web development include:\n- `vercel-labs/agent-skills` — React, Next.js, web design (100K+ installs each)\n- `anthropics/skills` — Frontend design, document processing (100K+ installs)\n\n### Step 3: Search for Skills\n\nIf the leaderboard doesn't cover the user's need, run the find command:\n\n```bash\nnpx skills find [query]\n```\n\nFor example:\n\n- User asks \"how do I make my React app faster?\" → `npx skills find react performance`\n- User asks \"can you help me with PR reviews?\" → `npx skills find pr review`\n- User asks \"I need to create a changelog\" → `npx skills find changelog`\n\n### Step 4: Verify Quality Before Recommending\n\n**Do not recommend a skill based solely on search results.** Always verify:\n\n1. **Install count** — Prefer skills with 1K+ installs. A skill from a repo with <100 stars should be treated with skepticism.\n\n### Step 5: Present Options to the User\n\nWhen you find relevant skills, present them to the user with:\n\n1. The skill name and what it does\n2. The install count and source\n3. The install command they can run\n4. Acknowledge that no existing skill was found\n2. Offer to help with the task directly using your general capabilities\n3. Suggest the user could create their own skill with `npx skills init`\n\nExample:\n\n```\nI searched for skills related to \"xyz\" but didn't find any matches.\nI can still help you with this task directly! Would you like me to proceed?\n\nIf this is something you do often, you could create your own skill:\nnpx skills init my-xyz-skill\n```\n</skill-instruction>\n\n<user-request>\n$ARGUMENTS\n</user-request>"
